<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Excellent findings:

**8B CED Scoring completed**:
- Total records: 24216
- Selected: 9041 (37.3% selection rate) — very consistent with LLaMA 8B (37.2%) and 1.7B (37.3%)
- Unique docs: 3027 total → 3018 in selected (9 docs dropped by f1 filter)
- Selected stats: format=1.0 (all selected have perfect format), f1=0.677 (higher than raw 0.595), ced=0.654 (higher than raw 0.562)


- Scoring used CED mode with tau=0.5 and top_k=3, where truncation affected 14.2% of records with a notable quality drop (F1 of 0.47 vs 0.62 for non-truncated)

**8B RSFT Training launched on GPU 3**: I'm starting the RSFT training pipeline using the Qwen3-8B base model with the SFT adapter, feeding it the scored training data and running for 3 epochs.

- Using bf16 LoRA (compliant with plan_006), batch size of 1 with 8 gradient accumulation steps for an effective batch of 8
- This gives roughly 3390 training steps across the 9041 samples
- Memory usage is hitting 31.2GB, which is actually tighter than the LLaMA setup even though the 8B model in bf16 should be around 16GB plus LoRA and optimizer overhead — something seems off with those numbers

I'm noticing the 8B command doesn't explicitly include the `--gradient_checkpointing` flag, whereas the LLaMA r3 spec shows it enabled, which brought memory down from 31.6GB to 22.9GB. The trainer might be enabling it by default, or it could be disabled entirely, which would explain why we're seeing 31.9GB without that optimization. cmdline doesn't have the flag. So the trainer might enable it by default for large models. The 8B Qwen uses 31.9GB (higher than LLaMA's 22.9GB). Wait, LLaMA is also 8B...

The difference could be:
- Qwen3-8B might be slightly larger (8.2B vs 8.0B)
- Different tokenizer/embedding sizes
- Gradient checkpointing might not be enabled for Qwen

This is concerning — 31.9/32.6 GB is 97.8% VRAM utilization. Very risky for OOM on longer sequences.

Actually wait, let me re-read. The LLaMA r3 is showing 22.9GB now, but it was 31.6GB before. The drop from 31.6→22.9 is probably because eval mode doesn't keep optimizer states around, whereas training forward and backward passes would use significantly more memory.

I'm not going to flag this yet since the process is running — if it hits OOM we'll catch it in the logs, but this is definitely a risk I should keep an eye on.

The 1.7B RSFT finished successfully with the final checkpoint at step 1698, completing all 3 epochs as expected. I need to pull the final evaluation metrics from that run. For the 8B RSFT on GPU 3, I'm verifying the configuration: GPU assignment is correct, bf16 LoRA is enabled per the plan, seed is set to 42.

Now I'm looking at the training data — 9041 samples from the CED scoring output. The concerning part is the memory usage at 97.8% without an explicit gradient checkpointing flag, which suggests the trainer might be relying on defaults. But I notice the worker name mentions "lead2" which could indicate prep work, yet what actually got submitted appears to be the 8B RSFT training that should follow the CED scoring. The registry doesn't show the 8B RSFT in the RUNNING list though, which is strange.

Looking at what's actually registered, I see the CED scoring is marked stale, and there are a few other jobs running, but the new 8B RSFT training isn't showing up yet — it might not be registered or the registry snapshot was outdated. Regardless, I should shift focus to checking the 1.7B completion metrics since that's what matters most right now.
```

```text
- Step 1698 (epoch 3, final): F1=0.6005, Ign-F1=0.5766, Evi-F1=0.7981, EDCR=0.5870 (840 preds)


- The grep output confirms these are the only two task evaluations in the logs, so I can see the full training trajectory. From step 566 to 1698, F1 improved by 3.1 percentage points and Ign-F1 by 3.0 percentage points, showing steady progress across the training run.
- However, evidence quality actually degraded significantly — Evi-F1 dropped 5.1 percentage points — while EDCR improved slightly and the model made fewer predictions overall by the end of training.
- Comparing against the baseline from D083, the 1.7B SFT model achieved Ign-F1 of 0.6319, but the RSFT variant's final Ign-F1 of 0.5766 suggests the reinforcement fine-tuning may have hurt performance on this metric despite the gains in standard F1.
- The discrepancy could stem from different evaluation protocols — D083 used a specific 442-document subset while the training eval uses only 50 documents — so these results might not be directly comparable.

Looking at the actual metrics from the final step, the training trajectory looks solid: F1 improved from 0.5695 to 0.6005, token accuracy reached 0.819, and loss decreased throughout training. The concerning part is the Evidence F1 dropping from 0.8494 down to 0.7981, which suggests the model might be struggling more with evidence extraction as training progresses.

To properly benchmark against the D083 baseline, I need to run a full dev evaluation on the final 1.7B RSFT checkpoint rather than relying on this 50-doc subset. The baseline showed 1.7B SFT achieving Ign-F1=0.6319 with a +6.2pp evidence tax, so I need to clarify whether that was measured on the same evaluation set and what the actual breakdown was between the SFT model with and without evidence constraints.

The 1.7B RSFT training completed in about 2 hours across 1698 steps, and while the task eval shows F1 improving over the training curve, the slight Evi-F1 degradation is worth monitoring. Meanwhile, the 8B CED scoring finished with 9041 examples selected from 24216 (37.3%), and I've launched 8B RSFT training on GPU 3 using bf16 LoRA — it's using 31.9GB so the memory is tight. GPU 1 is now available for other work.

I realize the "lead2_8b" job that ran was actually the 8B RSFT training step itself, which makes sense as a prerequisite for 线索2's RSFT→GRPO experiment — you need that trained checkpoint before moving into the GRPO phase.
```

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - **RSFT 3-param Loading Pattern**: `--model_path &lt;rsft_checkpoint> --base_model &lt;Qwen3-4B> --sft_adapter &lt;sft_output>` — auto-detects adapter, loads base, merges SFT, then loads RSFT LoRA
   - **CED (Contrastive Evidence Discrimination)**: NLI-based scoring using cross-encoder/nli-deberta-v3-base. Parameters: tau=0.5, top_k=3, scoring_mode=ced
   - **D076 Eval Protocol**: batch_size=4, --no-quantize, --sft_adapter, max_new_tokens=1024, seed=42
   - **D077**: plan_006 series must use bf16 LoRA, no QLoRA
   - **QLoRA OOM in PEFT _cast_adapter_dtype**: When PEFT loads an adapter on top of a quantized model, it tries to cast adapter weights to float32, which can OOM. Fix: use bf16 full precision instead (avoids the cast entirely)
   - **Evidence Tax**: Scale-dependent non-monotonic pattern — 1.7B benefits (+6.2pp), 4B pays (-7.5pp), 8B neutral
   - **Decoupling Spectrum (C2)**: RL optimization pressure correlates with evidence-extraction decoupling severity (GRPO-QLoRA catastrophic > GRPO-bf16 mild > DPO gradual > RSFT none)
   - **DocRED Test Blind Evaluation**: No local F1 computation; requires CodaLab submission
   - **CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES remapping**: Physical GPU N becomes logical cuda:0 for that process

```text
Critical finding! **lead2_rsft_grpo_ced crashed** with exit code 1. The crash is caused by:

1. `trust_remote_code` deprecation in the HuggingFace datasets library
2. Followed by `ConnectionError: Couldn't reach 'thunlp/docred' on the Hub (OfflineModeIsEnabled)`

The code at `docred_processor.py:129` tries to load the dataset from HuggingFace Hub with `trust_remote_code=True`, which:


- Is deprecated in newer versions of the `datasets` library
- Fails because the server is in offline mode

But here's the real problem: even though `--data_path /root/autodl-tmp/data/docred` is specified in the registry, the GRPO data pipeline is calling `load_dataset("thunlp/docred", ...)` directly from Hub instead of using the local data path. The model merge step did complete successfully though — base model, SFT adapter, and RSFT adapter all merged correctly and saved to `/root/autodl-tmp/merged_sft_rsft_`.

The crash happened during GRPO training when the dataset loader tried to fetch from Hub. I've traced it to a code bug in the GRPO pathway: `docred_processor.py` at line 129 calls `load_dataset()` from Hub instead of reading from the local `--data_path` argument. This is critical because the experiment is now dead with exit code 1, the GPU is freed, the registry still shows RUNNING, and while the fix is simple (just use the local data path), it requires worker intervention.

The merged checkpoint from the SFT/RSFT step exists successfully at `/root/autodl-tmp/merged_sft_rsft_ced_s43`, so the next attempt only needs to address the dataset loading bug. I'm wondering if this is a regression — previous GRPO experiments ran fine, so either the datasets library version changed or the GRPO code path has diverged from what worked before. The error message about `OfflineModeIsEnabled` suggests the server might have offline mode enabled via environment variables, which would block Hub access entirely. The old code used `trust_remote_code=True` which is now deprecated, so there's likely a two-part issue: the code tries to hit Hub (blocked in offline mode) and even without offline mode, the GRPO pathway isn't respecting the local data path argument.

The solution is straightforward — the GRPO data pipeline should load from the pre-downloaded local data at `/root/autodl-tmp/data/docred/` instead of trying to fetch from the Hub. I'm also noticing a tokenizer warning about Mistral regex patterns on what should be a Qwen3-4B model, which suggests the merged model might have an incorrect tokenizer config, but that's not causing the crash so I'll set it aside for now.
```

</details>

```json
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",
```

````text
```

So when `DocREDProcessor` is instantiated without `data_dir`, it falls through to the Hub fallback at line 135 where it tries to load from HuggingFace. The crash happens because `grpo_trainer.py` at line 328 creates the processor without passing `data_dir`. There's also an offline check that raises an error if those environment variables are set, but if they're not configured and the network call fails, that's where the crash occurs.

The real issue is that the worker never actually applied the fix — `docred_processor.py` still has the same problematic code at lines 135 and 295. The task was to fix the offline issue and submit lead2_r2, but neither of those things happened. The worker probably didn't have write access or didn't complete the work properly. The offline mode issue might not be solvable through environment variables alone since it's a dataset-level configuration. Let me double-check whether the GRPO trainer was actually modified to pass the data directory correctly, since my earlier search didn't find evidence of that change.
````
